# SQL SELECT fidelity audit — November 2026

Requirement for this release: a query returns exactly what its text says, or fails with a clear "not supported"
error — never a silently wrong result. This audit measured the current engine against that requirement on a live
Dataverse environment.

## Summary

| | Cases |
|---|---|
| Run | 436 |
| Correct | 238 |
| **WRONG RESULT** (silently wrong rows, values, order or columns) | 129 |
| Clean error (an error, acceptable under the requirement; some messages are poor) | 62 |
| **Crash** | 7 (6 unbounded-recursion stack overflows that kill the process, 1 raw `FormatException`) |

The engine does not meet the requirement. The most dangerous defects drop or invert filters without saying so. The
WHERE clause of UPDATE and DELETE is planned through the same SELECT planner
(`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Dml.cs` builds a synthetic `SELECT … WHERE …`), so every WHERE defect below also changes
which rows an UPDATE or DELETE touches. For the marked cases a DELETE dry-run was planned (no execution) and its
FetchXML and plan were recorded. In several of them the planned DELETE has **no filter at all**, so it would delete
every row in the table.

## Method

- **Harness**: `tests/PPDS.LiveTests/Query/SelectFidelityLiveTests.cs` (lifecycle, runner, OBSERVED probes, report),
  `SelectFidelityLiveTests.Cases.cs` (the case catalogue), `SelectFidelity/TSql.cs` (the independent reference
  semantics) and `SelectFidelity/ResultComparer.cs`. The test is `Category=Integration`, runs on .NET 8 only, and is
  skipped unless the live-test profile settings are present (`MetadataLiveFact`).
- **Data**: the test creates three throwaway tables in a disposable unmanaged solution:
  - a parent table with 36 rows. Its columns are string (primary name), memo, two whole numbers, decimal, money,
    float, boolean, user-local datetime, date-only, choice, multi-select choice, and two strings `ppds_itemtype` and
    `ppds_itemtypename`. The second is a real column whose name ends in `name`.
  - a child table with 24 rows and a lookup to the parent.
  - a bulk table with 5,050 rows, which crosses one 5,000-row FetchXML page.

  The data includes NULLs in every nullable column, duplicates, mixed case, accented strings, leading and trailing
  spaces, `%`, `_`, `[` and `'` characters, negative and zero numbers, boundary dates (1900, 2099, 29 February),
  times near UTC midnight, and choice labels whose alphabetical order differs from their value order.
- **Truth**: after seeding, the rows are read back through the SDK with plain `QueryExpression`, never through PPDS.
  That copy is the in-memory truth, so any normalisation the server applies on save is included.
- **Expected results** come from that in-memory copy. They are plain C#/LINQ implementing T-SQL semantics:
  - three-valued logic;
  - NULL-first ordering;
  - the organisation's collation, probed live (case- and accent-insensitive), with trailing-space padding for `=`;
  - LIKE with `%`, `_`, `[...]`, `[^...]` and `ESCAPE`;
  - T-SQL integer division and AVG truncation;
  - NULL-skipping aggregates.

  No PPDS code computes any expected value.
- **Actual results**: each statement runs through the real `SqlQueryService.ExecuteAsync`, the same path `ppds query
  sql` uses, with `DmlSafety` set as the CLI sets it. The result is read the way the CLI prints it: column
  `Alias ?? LogicalName`, raw `QueryValue.Value`.
- **Comparison**: the comparator checks column names and order, then the rows (a multiset, or a sequence when the
  statement has a total ORDER BY), then the values (numbers to 4 dp, datetimes in UTC, GUIDs, strings exactly; strings
  by collation only for DISTINCT, GROUP BY and UNION over case variants). Two things are recorded but not counted as
  failures in every case, because each is reported once below as its own defect:
  - the documented `*name` auto-expansion column after a lookup, choice or boolean column;
  - the unrequested primary-key column.

  The shape cases PRJ01, PRJ13 and PRJ22 check those two strictly. A key missing from a record counts as NULL. A zero-row
  result may report no columns, as specs/query.md documents. Each case records correct, WRONG RESULT, clean error or
  crash, and a failure never stops the run.
- **OBSERVED** lines record raw Dataverse behaviour, measured with hand-written FetchXML sent directly through the SDK,
  not through PPDS.
- **Process crashes**: six statements make the planner recurse without bound. The resulting stack overflow ends the
  process and cannot be caught, so in-process they are recorded as crashes without being run. They were reproduced
  planner-only, in a child process with no Dataverse call. All six statements, plus the equivalent DELETE, exit with
  `0xC00000FD` "Stack overflow"; a top-level `IN (SELECT …)` plans normally.
- The case list was taken from the planner and compiler, not from `specs/query.md`, which lists far less than the
  planner accepts. The planner and compiler sources are `ExecutionPlanBuilder*`, `FetchXmlGenerator`,
  `ExpressionCompiler`, `FunctionRegistry`, `ScriptExecutionNode` and `ClientWindowNode`.

## Environment and date

- 2026-11-17. Organisation `<development organisation>`, connected through the signed-in
  InteractiveBrowser profile `PPDS` with prompts refused. The connected-organisation check passed on every connection.
- Caller time zone: UTC−6. The formatted value of 2024-01-15T00:30:00Z is `1/14/2024 6:30 PM`.
- Organisation collation as observed: case-insensitive, accent-insensitive, trailing spaces ignored by `eq`, leading
  spaces significant.
- Branch `test/select-fidelity-live`, from main at 3c49397fb. Five live runs were made. Runs 1–4 found and fixed harness issues:
  - run 1 hit the stack overflow and lost its in-process cleanup; its tables were then deleted by hand;
  - in later runs, unnamed columns blocked the documented-column filter, absent keys were not treated as NULL, and a
    table creation collided with a concurrent run elsewhere in the organisation.

  The table below is run 5. Its verdicts are identical to run 3, apart from the harness fixes.

## Silent wrong results, most dangerous first

"DML" says whether the defect also changes the rows an UPDATE or DELETE touches. Every WHERE defect does, because
of the synthetic-SELECT planning in `ExecutionPlanBuilder.Dml.cs` (lines 124-161 for UPDATE, 195-223 for DELETE).
"Confirmed" means a DELETE dry-run was planned and its recorded FetchXML or plan shows the same defect.

1. **Predicates whose left side is not a bare column, or whose right side is not a literal, are dropped — the filter
   disappears.** This covers `LIKE`, `IN`, `BETWEEN` and `IS NULL` on an expression (`UPPER(name) LIKE 'A%'`,
   `ISNULL(name,'') IN (…)`, `LEN(name) BETWEEN 2 AND 3`, `COALESCE(a,b) IS NULL`), a column or variable pattern
   (`name LIKE itemtype + '%'`, `name LIKE @pat`), and a column bound (`num BETWEEN num2 AND 10`, `num IN (num2, 100)`).
   - **Returned**: every row (W76, W77, W97, W98, W99, X11). With a column inside IN or BETWEEN, the column is sent as
     `<value></value>`, so the query returns 0 or 1 rows instead of 10 or 5 (W136, W137). Wrong COUNT(*) (A30).
   - **Cause**: `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1227-1228`, `1267`, `1282`, `1367` and the inner variants at `1404-1500`
     silently `return`/`break`. `FormatLiteralValue` returns `""` for any non-literal (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1720-1721`).
     `ExtractClientSideWhereFilter` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Helpers.cs:573-619`) only recognises
     `BooleanComparisonExpression`, so nothing is evaluated client-side.
   - **DML**: yes, confirmed. W76, W77 and W97–W99 plan `DELETE` with a FetchXML that has no filter.
   - **Fix**: every predicate the generator cannot push must either be evaluated client-side (extend
     `ExtractClientSideWhereFilter` to all predicate types whose operands are not column/literal) or throw
     `NotSupportedException`. The `return`/`break` statements and the `""` default must become exceptions.
2. **`NOT IN (subquery)` is ignored — every row is returned.**
   - **Returned**: S15 `key NOT IN (SELECT key FROM C WHERE qty > 0)` returns all 36 rows (expected 23). S03 and S14
     also return all 36 rows; T-SQL returns none, because the subquery yields a NULL.
   - **Cause**: `ExecutionPlanBuilder.Join.cs:165-170` rewrites the FetchXML into a left-outer anti-join, but only the
     returned `FetchXml` string changes. The scan node keeps the original FetchXML (`currentNode = outerResult.RootNode`,
     line 173).
   - **DML**: yes, confirmed. The DELETE dry-run displays the anti-join FetchXML, but its plan is
     `DmlExecute ← FetchXmlScan` with no filter. The preview misleads, and execution would delete every row.
   - **Fix**: build the scan node from the rewritten FetchXML, or drop the rewrite. In either case honour T-SQL NULL
     semantics: when the subquery can return NULL, `NOT IN` matches nothing. Without a NOT NULL guarantee, use the
     HashSemiJoin path with a NULL check.
3. **Conditions on a joined table's columns are applied to the primary table.**
   - **Returned**:
     - J04 `LEFT JOIN … WHERE c.key IS NULL` returns 0 rows (expected the 27 childless parents);
     - J23 `FROM C c JOIN P p … WHERE p.name LIKE 'a%'` filters on the child's name;
     - J16 `COUNT(c.key)` counts the parent's key, so childless parents report 1;
     - J21 raised a Dataverse fault only because the parent lacks `ppds_qty`.
   - **Cause**: `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1213` and `1397` use `GetColumnName` and drop the qualifier, so no
     `entityname=` is emitted. The same applies to ORDER BY (`1606`) and aggregates (`772`).
   - **DML**: yes, for `DELETE … FROM … JOIN … WHERE alias.col …`.
   - **Fix**: resolve the qualifier, then emit `entityname="<alias>"` on the condition or order, or place the
     condition inside the link-entity. An unknown qualifier must be an error.
4. **`NOT (…)` loses its NOT.**
   - **Returned**:
     - `NOT (num = 5)` and `NOT num = 5` return the rows where `num = 5` (W09, W10);
     - `NOT (name IS NULL)` returns the NULL rows (W29); W31 is the mirror case;
     - `NOT (a AND b)`, `NOT (a OR b)` and `NOT (x IN (…))` all return the un-negated set (W44–W48);
     - W11 and W12 are wrong in the same way.
   - **Cause**: the default branch of `EmitNotFilter` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1353-1356`, "just emit inner"). A
     parenthesised inner expression never reaches the IS NULL or IN special cases.
   - **DML**: yes, confirmed (W09, W29, W44 DELETE dry-runs show the un-negated filter).
   - **Fix**: push NOT down by De Morgan with NULL-aware operators: `NOT (a = v)` becomes `a <> v AND a IS NOT NULL`,
     and `NOT (a IS NULL)` becomes `not-null`. Otherwise evaluate client-side with three-valued logic, or throw.
5. **Negative operators match NULLs.**
   - **Returned**: `<>` and `!=` return the NULL rows (W02, W03, W19, W120). So do `NOT IN` (W50, W55), `NOT LIKE`
     (W68, W75) and `<> NULL`, which returns all 36 rows (W25). `NOT IN (5, NULL)` returns the NULL rows; T-SQL
     returns nothing (W52).
   - **Cause**: Dataverse evaluates `ne`, `not-in`, `not-like`, `not-begin-with` and `not-end-with` as true for NULL
     (OBSERVED below). `not-between` does exclude NULLs. The engine maps the operators one-to-one
     (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1565-1566`, `1290`, `1238-1255`).
   - **DML**: yes, confirmed (W02, W50, W52, W68 dry-runs).
   - **Fix**: emit `<filter type="and"><condition … operator="ne"/><condition … operator="not-null"/></filter>` for
     each negative operator. `<> NULL`, `= NULL` and a NULL inside `NOT IN` must match nothing.
6. **An expression comparison inside OR, or inside an AND nested under OR, is dropped from the server filter.**
   - **Returned**: `num = num2 OR name = 'Beta'` returns only the Beta row (W89). `num2 IS NULL OR num = num2` is
     missing 4 rows (W138).
   - **Cause**: `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1395` skips the condition inside a logical filter. The server then applies the
     narrower `OR`, and the client re-filters only what is left.
   - **DML**: yes, confirmed (W89).
   - **Fix**: when any disjunct is client-side, emit no server filter for that whole OR subtree.
     `ExtractClientSideWhereFilter` already returns the whole OR for client evaluation.
7. **Variables, `= NULL` and `<> NULL` are sent as literal text.**
   - **Returned**: `DECLARE @n … = 'Alpha'; … WHERE name = @n` returns 0 rows (X01); the FetchXML value is the text
     `@n`. With an int column, X02, X03 and X12 fail with a Dataverse format error that mentions no variable.
     X05, the datetime variant, fails the same way. `= NULL` is sent as `eq ""`; it returns 0 rows here only because
     Dataverse stores no empty strings. `<> NULL` returns every row.
   - **Cause**: `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1709-1710` (NULL becomes `""`) and `1715-1718` (the variable name).
     `ScriptExecutionNode` passes the variable scope into the planner options (line 1034), but the generator never
     reads it.
   - **DML**: yes.
   - **Fix**: substitute variable values from `QueryPlanOptions.VariableScope` before generation, or evaluate those
     predicates client-side. `= NULL` and `<> NULL` must match nothing.
8. **TOP, `--top` and `MAX_ROWS` are applied before the client-side filter, and some TOP forms are ignored.**
   - **Returned**:
     - `SELECT TOP 3 … WHERE num = num2` returns 1 row instead of 3 (T07); `--top 5` on the same filter returns 3
       instead of 4 (PG11).
     - `TOP (5)` returns all 36 rows (T02).
     - `TOP 50 PERCENT` returns 36 rows (T05); FetchXML `top="50"` is sent.
   - **Cause**:
     - The scan's `maxRows` comes from `options.MaxRows ?? top` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.cs:250`) and is applied before
       the `ClientFilterNode` (`265-274`).
     - The parenthesised form fails because `ExtractTopValue` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:219-230`) and
       `ExtractTopFromQuerySpec` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Helpers.cs:796-804`) accept only an `IntegerLiteral`.
     - PERCENT and WITH TIES are never read.
   - **DML**: no, because DML does not use TOP.
   - **Fix**: when any client-side node exists, apply TOP or MaxRows as an `OffsetFetchNode` above it. Unwrap the
     `ParenthesisExpression`. PERCENT and WITH TIES must throw unless implemented.
9. **Datetime literals without an offset are interpreted in the caller's time zone.**
   - **Returned**: `dt >= '2024-01-15'` misses the 00:30Z row (W101). `dt = '2024-01-15 00:30:00'` returns nothing
     (W103). The engine itself returns the UTC value.
   - **Cause**: FetchXML interprets offset-less values in the user's zone (OBSERVED). The generator passes the
     literal unchanged (`FormatLiteralValue`, `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1697-1698`).
   - **DML**: yes.
   - **Fix**: parse datetime literals compared with datetime columns and send them with an explicit `Z` (UTC), so
     they match the values the engine returns. Or document local interpretation and convert the returned values to
     match.
10. **Computed columns read their inputs as NULL.**
    - **Returned**:
      - `COALESCE`, `NULLIF`, simple `CASE`, `TRY_CAST` and `TRY_CONVERT` over unselected columns (FN61, FN62, FN67,
        FN70, FN86, FN87);
      - every `CASE WHEN col > literal` and `IIF(col > literal, …)` (FN63–FN66, FN71);
      - `name AS n` combined with a computed column returns `n` as NULL (PRJ05, PRJ21);
      - `num AS n, num + 1 AS m` returns both as NULL (PRJ20);
      - window `PARTITION BY` / `ORDER BY` columns are never fetched, so RANK is 1 for every row and ROW_NUMBER ignores
        the partition (WF02, WF03, WF07, WF11, WF13);
      - joined-table operands are NULL (J17).
    - **Cause**:
      - `CollectColumnsFromScalar` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:992-1046`) has no case for `CoalesceExpression`,
        `NullIfExpression`, `SimpleCaseExpression`, `TryCastCall` or `TryConvertCall`;
      - `CollectExpressionConditionColumns` (`933-977`) collects a predicate's columns only for column-to-column or
        computed comparisons, so `col > 5` inside CASE or IIF fetches nothing;
      - an aliased column marks its name as emitted (`735`), so the expression's input is never fetched under its own
        name, while `ProjectNode` renames by source name (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Helpers.cs:965`);
      - `BuildWindowNodeFromScriptDom` (`Helpers.cs:846-939`) never asks the generator for its columns.
    - **DML**: the computed `SET` values of UPDATE use the same compiler. Not tested.
    - **Fix**: collect columns with one `TSqlFragmentVisitor` over every scalar and boolean node of the SELECT list,
      WHERE, ORDER BY and window clauses. Always fetch base columns unaliased and apply aliases in the projection.
11. **Client-side evaluation does not follow T-SQL semantics.**
    - **Returned**:
      - `NOT` is two-valued: `NOT (num = num2)` includes the NULL rows (W90), and `CASE WHEN NOT (x > 5)` takes the
        THEN branch for NULL (FN66);
      - string comparison is ordinal-ignore-case, not the accent-insensitive, trailing-space-padded collation
        (W92, W100, FN68, FN69);
      - `datetime > 'literal'` compares strings, so `GETUTCDATE() > '2020-01-01'` is false (FN52) and
        `CAST(dt AS date) = '2024-01-15'` matches nothing (W110);
      - `LIKE … ESCAPE` is ignored server-side (W60, W74): the escape character is sent as a literal backslash.
    - **Cause**:
      - `ExpressionCompiler.cs:656-660` (NOT);
      - `561-563` (comparison returns false, not unknown);
      - `727-728` and `770-772` (string and fallback comparison);
      - `MatchLikePattern` `895-935` (no `[...]`, no ESCAPE);
      - `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1225-1260` (ESCAPE never read).
    - **DML**: yes, for client-side predicates.
    - **Fix**: make predicates return `bool?` and propagate unknown. Compare with
      `CompareInfo(IgnoreCase|IgnoreNonSpace)` after trimming trailing spaces. Convert string literals to the other
      operand's type. Support ESCAPE, translating to `[_]` / `[%]` for FetchXML, or throw.
12. **UNION, INTERSECT and EXCEPT are wrong.**
    - **Returned**:
      - UNION does not deduplicate (U01, U05, U08); INTERSECT returns 0 rows (U03, U09); EXCEPT returns everything
        (U04);
      - columns of the second branch onward keep their own names, so their values appear as NULL under the first
        branch's column (U02, U06, U07);
      - `ORDER BY` after a UNION is ignored (U06);
      - `A UNION B UNION ALL C` deduplicates C as well (U05).
    - **Cause**:
      - the composite row key includes the hidden primary-key column, the column names and ordinal-case values
        (`DistinctNode.cs:57-70`, `IntersectNode.cs:87-100`, `ExceptNode`);
      - `PlanUnion` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Statements.cs:127-182`) neither renames branch columns nor reads the
        `OrderByClause`;
      - line 169 applies DISTINCT to all branches if any boundary is UNION.
    - **Fix**: project every branch to the first branch's column names, positionally. Build keys from the projected
      values only, using collation-aware equality. Honour ORDER BY, OFFSET and TOP on the union. Respect boundary
      precedence.
13. **SELECT \* drops columns that are NULL in the first returned row.**
    - **Returned**: across runs, `SELECT *` lost `ppds_num2`, `ppds_money`, `ppds_colorcode`, `ppds_itemtype` and
      `ppds_itemtypename` (run 2), and `ppds_memo` (run 5), for all 36 rows (PRJ02). For a single row, every NULL
      column is missing (PRJ03).
    - **Cause**: `PlanExecutor.cs:40-44` infers the columns from the first row's keys only. Dataverse omits NULL
      attributes. `QueryExecutor.InferColumnsFromRecords` scans every row, but its result is not used.
    - **Fix**: use the executor's column list (the union over all pages), or the entity metadata, for `*`.
14. **The result has columns the statement did not ask for, or lacks columns it did.**
    - **Extra columns**:
      - every non-computed SELECT adds the primary key, e.g. `[ppds_key, ppds_name, ppds_sqlp…id]`
        (PRJ01, PRJ13, PRJ22). Cause: `QueryExecutor.cs:606-610` plus first-row inference;
      - whole-number, decimal, money and float columns get a bogus `<col>name` column holding the formatted number,
        e.g. `ppds_keyname = '7'`. Cause: `QueryValue.IsOptionSet` (`QueryValue.cs:82`) is true for any `int` with a
        formatted value, and `SqlQueryResultExpander` then expands it;
      - columns used only in a client-side WHERE are added to the output (W86–W96, W109, T07, T11). Cause:
        `EmitExpressionConditionColumns` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:909-927`) adds attributes and nothing projects them
        away.
    - **Merged or renamed columns**:
      - unnamed computed columns are all named `computed` and collapse into one (PRJ15;
        `ExecutionPlanBuilder.Helpers.cs:995`);
      - `SELECT key, key` returns one column (PRJ12);
      - an alias on a virtual column is ignored (V15).
    - **Fix**: always finish with a projection to exactly the SELECT list. Restrict auto-expansion to values whose
      source attribute is a lookup, choice or boolean, using metadata or the SDK type, not `int` with formatting.
15. **Virtual `*name` detection misreads real columns.**
    - **Returned**:
      - `SELECT ppds_itemtypename`, a real string column, returns `''` or NULL, because it is read as the formatted
        value of `ppds_itemtype` (V03, V16);
      - `ppds_flagname`, the boolean label of a custom column, is not detected; Dataverse accepts the attribute and
        silently returns NULL (V04);
      - `systemuser.domainname` fails with "doesn't contain attribute domain" (V07, a clean error but wrong).
    - **Cause**: `IsVirtualNameColumn` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:458-494`) is a pure suffix and prefix heuristic.
    - **Fix**: decide with metadata: a column is virtual only when `<base>` exists, is a lookup, choice or boolean, and
      `<base>name` is not a real attribute. Without metadata, treat it as a real column.
16. **Client-side join path: RIGHT, FULL and CROSS joins, and cross-table expressions.**
    - **Returned**:
      - `p.ppds_key` and `c.ppds_key` resolve to the same value, so `RIGHT JOIN` returns `(16,16)` pairs (J09);
      - FULL returns 60 rows instead of 51 (J10); CROSS JOIN with a WHERE returns 48 rows instead of 6 (J11);
      - `c.num = p.num` compares a column with itself (J24);
      - `ON … AND c.qty > 0` emits `from="" to=""` (J08, 36 rows instead of 39);
      - duplicate column names come back in FetchXML order, not SELECT order (J13).
    - **Cause**: the qualified fallback in `ExpressionCompiler.CompileColumnReference` (`224-265`) resolves `alias.col`
      to whichever `col` key it finds. `ExtractJoinColumns` returns `("", "")` for any ON that is not a single equality
      (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:351`).
    - **Fix**: give rows from each join side alias-qualified keys and require qualified resolution. A compound ON
      condition must be implemented or refused.
17. **ORDER BY: expressions and ordinals are ignored; choices sort by label.**
    - **Returned**: `ORDER BY num * -1` and `ORDER BY 2, 1` return the rows in a different order, unsorted by the
      requested key (O04, O05). `TOP 2 … ORDER BY LEN(name)` returns the wrong rows (T08). `ORDER BY colorcode`
      sorts by label (Blue, Green, Red), not by value (O07).
    - **Cause**: `EmitOrderBy` returns silently for any non-column (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:1604`). FetchXML orders
      choices by label (OBSERVED).
    - **Fix**: when any ORDER BY item is not a plain column, sort client-side (`BuildClientSortNode` already exists).
      Choices must be sorted by value, or documented.
18. **Aggregates.**
    - **Returned**:
      - over zero rows, `SUM` and `MAX` return 0, not NULL (A10);
      - `STRING_AGG(…) WITHIN GROUP (…)` is treated as a scalar and returns one row per input row (FN91);
      - `DISTINCT num % 2` returns 22 rows, not 4 (D08): FetchXML DISTINCT runs on the base column and the
        computation is applied afterwards.
    - **Cause**:
      - A10: unverified whether Dataverse or the mapping turns NULL into 0;
      - FN91: `STRING_AGG` is absent from `IsAggregateFunction` (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:526-539`);
      - D08: `FetchXmlGenerator.cs:88-91`, with no DistinctNode after the projection.
    - **Fix**: map empty-input aggregates to NULL. Treat STRING_AGG as an aggregate, or refuse it. Use a client
      DistinctNode after any computed projection.
19. **CTEs.**
    - **Returned**: the recursive CTE `n` (1..5) returns `(1)` and one NULL row (C03). A CTE with the same name as
      the table is silently ignored, so it returns all rows; T-SQL raises an error (C04).
    - **Cause**: `PlanWithCtes` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Statements.cs:216-227`) never binds non-recursive CTEs.
      RecursiveCteNode projection.
    - **Fix**: materialise each CTE and bind it in `CteBindings`. Until then, any CTE reference must be refused.
20. **Silently ignored clauses.**
    - **Returned**: `FOR JSON PATH` returns ordinary rows (E13). `COLLATE Latin1_General_CS_AS` is ignored, so the
      comparison stays case-insensitive (W134, W135). A multi-statement script returns only the last result set (X08).
    - **Fix**: throw for `ForClause` and for any `Collation` the engine does not apply. X08 must be documented, or
      return all result sets.
21. **String-function edge semantics** (low).
    - **Returned**: `STUFF(s, 2, 1, 'X')` on a one-character string returns `'bX'`; T-SQL returns NULL (FN17).
      REPLACE, CHARINDEX and PATINDEX are case-insensitive but accent-sensitive (FN10–FN12), unlike the
      organisation's collation.

### Known-at-start items not reproduced as stated

- `LIKE '[ab]%'` is correct (W62). Dataverse treats the `begins-with` value as a LIKE prefix pattern, so the bracket
  class works. By the same token, `begins-with 'a_'` treats `_` as a wildcard, as OBSERVED.
- `WHERE name = NULL` returns 0 rows here (W24), but only because Dataverse stores no empty strings. `<> NULL` is
  wrong (W25), and so is any non-string `= NULL` that Dataverse might coerce.
- The `IsVirtualNameColumn` misclassification is confirmed. On `systemuser.domainname` it shows as an error, not a
  silent value (V07). On a custom column whose base also exists it is silent (V03).

## Crashes

- **Unbounded recursion: stack overflow ends the process.** This happens for `IN (subquery)` or `EXISTS` anywhere
  other than a top-level AND conjunct: inside OR, inside parentheses, or under NOT
  (S05, S16, S18, S19, S20, S21, and `DELETE … WHERE a = 5 OR k IN (SELECT …)`).
  - **Cause**: `ExtractInSubqueryPredicates` (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.Join.cs:476-478`) and `ExtractExistsPredicates`
    (`629-630`) leave the predicate in place, so `PlanSelect` routes back into
    `PlanInSubquery` / `PlanExistsSubquery` forever.
  - **Impact**: the CLI exits with `0xC00000FD`. The daemon, TUI or MCP server would die with it.
  - **Fix**: if extraction leaves a subquery predicate behind, throw a clear "IN/EXISTS subquery inside OR/NOT is not
    supported" error. A recursion guard is cheap.
- **Raw `FormatException`.** `CAST(name AS int)` over non-numeric data surfaces as
  "The input string 'lead' was not in a correct format." (E04). T-SQL errors too, so the outcome is right, but the
  exception is unwrapped and gives no column or value context.

## Features that fail with an error (acceptable under the requirement; messages worth improving)

- **GROUP BY on any column** fails: "An alias must be specified for every attribute in an aggregate query"
  (A08, A09, A11–A14, A18, A19, A23, A31, PG14). The group-by attribute is emitted without an alias
  (`FetchXmlGenerator.cs:725-733`). This blocks a feature specs/query.md lists as supported.
  - `GROUP BY` with no aggregate (E14) and `-- ppds:HASH_GROUP` (A27) fail: "groupby cannot be specified if aggregate
    is not true".
  - `GROUP BY YEAR(dt)` (A15), `SUM(expr)` (A17), an aggregate combined with a client-side WHERE (A21), and
    `MIN`/`MAX` on strings and choices (A06, A25) fail with Dataverse faults.
- **Aggregate window functions** (`SUM`/`COUNT`/`AVG`/`MIN`/`MAX OVER`) fail: "An attribute can not be requested when
  an aggregate operation has been specified" (WF04–WF06, WF09, WF10, WF14). `LAG` is "not supported" (WF12).
- **Subqueries**:
  - correlated `EXISTS` and `NOT EXISTS` fail: "'C' entity doesn't contain attribute … ppds_sqlp…id" (S06–S08);
  - scalar subqueries in SELECT, WHERE and IF fail: "ScalarSubquery is not yet supported" (S09, S10, S17, X06);
  - non-recursive CTEs fail: "entity 'x' was not found" (C01, C02, C05).
- **Date functions with a datepart argument** (`DATEPART(year, …)`, `DATEADD`, `DATEDIFF`, `DATENAME`, `DATETRUNC`)
  fail: "'P' entity doesn't contain attribute … 'year'". The datepart is fetched as a column
  (FN43–FN48, FN51; `CollectColumnsFromScalar` treats it as a column reference).
- **LEFT() and RIGHT()** are "not yet supported by ExpressionCompiler" (FN04, FN05).
- **Statements without a FROM clause** (`SELECT 1 AS x`, `SELECT 1 + 1`) fail: "Cannot determine entity name"
  (E05, X15).
- **Variables**: scripts whose variable reaches the client-side compiler fail: "Variable @i cannot be resolved: no
  VariableScope is configured" (X04, X07, X14). The cause is that the planner's `ExpressionCompiler` is created
  without a scope accessor (`ExecutionPlanBuilder.cs:49`). `SELECT @v = MAX(…)` reports "Function 'MAX' is not
  supported" (X09).
- **Temp tables** fail: "SELECT INTO #temp requires a SessionContext" (X10). `SqlQueryService` builds
  `ExecutionPlanBuilder` without a `SessionContext` (`SqlQueryService.cs:89`), so the CLI cannot use temp tables
  at all.
- **Other errors**:
  - an alias with a space fails with a Dataverse alias rule error (PRJ18);
  - `ORDER BY <alias>` fails with "doesn't contain attribute 'n'" (O03);
  - a comma join fails with a Dataverse fault (J18);
  - `5.0` against an int column fails with a Dataverse format error (W15);
  - `'20240115'` is rejected by Dataverse (W104).
- **Errors that are correct** because T-SQL errors too: invalid column or table (E01, E02, E07), divide by zero
  (E03), `num = 'abc'` (E06), a non-grouped column (A29) and `CONTAINS` (E11).

## Not tested, and why

- **`--tds` / `USE_TDS`.** Building a `TdsQueryExecutor` means handing the run an access token, which the live-test
  rules forbid. The TDS endpoint is also an organisation setting that must not be changed. Untested.
- **Cross-environment reads (`[LABEL].entity`).** There is only one environment. By code reading, the WHERE is applied
  client-side after a full remote scan. The results look correct but can be slow. Not run.
- **DML execution.** Only DELETE dry-runs were planned, to show which filter DML would use; nothing was updated or
  deleted through the SQL engine. Because the WHERE path is shared, every WHERE defect applies to UPDATE and DELETE.
  The computed `SET` expressions of UPDATE were not exercised.
- **Paging.** Single-table paging across a page boundary was tested and is correct: auto-paging over 5,050 rows,
  `--page` with and without `--paging-cookie`, TOP above 5,000, DISTINCT, a client filter and COUNT across pages
  (PG01–PG08). Not tested:
  - paging with link-entities, where the parent-merge logic in `FetchXmlScanNode` applies;
  - `--top` combined with `--page`;
  - `IncludeCount`;
  - prefetch (`EnablePrefetch`);
  - aggregate partitioning, which needs more than 50,000 rows.
- **Other untested features**:
  - multi-select choices beyond `IS NULL` and plain selection (V14, W39, both correct);
  - MERGE, cursors, `EXECUTE AS`, `EXECUTE` of messages, metadata virtual tables, `OPENJSON`/`STRING_SPLIT`, most JSON
    functions, `RAND`/`NEWID` (non-deterministic), and the `BYPASS_*` / `BATCH_SIZE` hints.
- **Single setting.** The time-zone result was measured for one caller time zone (UTC−6) and one collation (the
  organisation's).

## Development environment afterwards

- **Created and confirmed deleted**: the disposable unmanaged solution `ppds_SelectLive20261117` under
  `PPDSDemoPublisher`, prefix `ppds`. Each run created three tables in it.
  - **Run 1**: `ppds_sqlpf13a82`, `ppds_sqlcf13a82`, `ppds_sqlbf13a82`. The stack overflow killed the test process
    before its cleanup ran. The three tables, and their rows with them, were deleted with the scratch setup tool's
    restricted `delete-table` command. That command accepts only `ppds_sql[pcb]` plus 6 hex characters, and only
    for a component of the solution.
  - **Runs 2, 3 and 5**: `ppds_sqlp/c/b680161`, `ppds_sqlp/c/b31f7c3` and `ppds_sqlp/c/b14fd83`. The test deleted
    the parent and child rows itself, then deleted each table and confirmed it was gone by `RetrieveEntity`. The bulk
    rows were removed with their table.
  - **Run 4**: `ppds_sqlp992fd6` was never created, because Dataverse reported that another table creation was in
    progress.
- **Final leftovers scan**: no entity starting `ppds_sql`, and 0 solution components; the solution was then deleted.
  Publishing was limited to the run's own three tables. No organisation or user setting was changed. The only
  standard table read was the caller's own `systemuser` row.
